Instead, my general feelings of sadness and lack of energy have just been relatively consistent for a little over a year now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical day for you, including how you feel when you wake up, and if there are any activities or things that still bring you some pleasure or relief from your depressive feelings?\n\nPatient: The moment I wake up, I'm hit with a heavy, low mood and absolutely no motivation to even get out of bed. My typical day is just a flat, empty routine because absolutely nothing brings me any pleasure, relief, or joy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have there been any periods lasting 2 months or more where you didn't experience your depressive symptoms, or have they been continuously present for the entire duration?\n\nPatient: My symptoms actually started about a year and a month ago, and they have been continuously present without any breaks since then. Prior to that, I didn't experience these depressive feelings at all. So, I haven't had any symptom-free periods since they first began 13 months ago.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there were any specific events, stressors, or changes in your life that occurred around the time your depressive symptoms started, about a year and a month ago?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't recall any specific life events, stressors, or changes that triggered these feelings. I have been experiencing these depressive symptoms continuously for about a year and a month now without any real breaks. I also don't recall ever having an episode like this in my past.\n\nDoctor: To finalize your diagnosis, can you confirm again that you've had a continuous period of depressive symptoms for about a year and a month, with no significant breaks or periods of feeling better, and you don't recall any similar long-lasting depressive episodes in your past?\n\nPatient: Yes, I confirm that. I've been experiencing these depressive symptoms continuously for about a year and a month with no breaks or times where I felt better. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
